Other states similarly had state-authorized extensions beyond November 3rd by law. These extensions were not due to a declared federal emergency.

### What were the historical origins and primary promoters of the claim that 5G technology causes COVID-19?

### What are the fundamental differences between ionizing radiation (like X-rays) and non-ionizing radiation (like that used by 5G) in terms of their known effects on human biology?
Answer: **CONFLICT.** The retrieved document store contained no material bearing on the fundamental differences between ionizing and non-ionizing radiation. The reasoner working from memory, which is nearly certain of its account, reports that ionizing radiation like X-rays has sufficient photon energy to eject electrons from atoms and break chemical bonds—including those in DNA—which can cause mutations, cellular damage, and increased cancer risk even at low doses, whereas non-ionizing radiation like the radio waves used by 5G lacks the photon energy to ionize atoms or break molecular bonds and its only well-established effect on human tissue at telecommunications power levels is negligible thermal heating; because non-ionizing radiation cannot directly damage DNA, it has no established mechanism for causing infections or viruses like SARS-CoV-2, making the premise of the original claim scientifically baseless.

### When were the World Bank and the International Monetary Fund (IMF) officially established, and at what specific event or conference did this occur?
Answer: The World Bank and the International Monetary Fund (IMF) were established at the United Nations Monetary and Financial Conference (the Bretton Woods Conference), held July 1–22, 1944, in Bretton Woods, New Hampshire, which laid the foundations for both institutions; their articles of agreement were subsequently ratified by member nations in 1945, at which point they became operationally effective.

Source URL: https://pubdocs.worldbank.org/en/634901541019944926/70-years-prologue.pdf

### What was the political status and governing authority of the Philippines during the year 1944?

Source URL: https://constitutionnet.org/country/philippines

### Which nations and delegates are documented as having participated in the 1944 Bretton Woods Conference that founded the World Bank and IMF?
Answer: The 1944 Bretton Woods Conference, which established the World Bank and International Monetary Fund, was attended by delegates from 44 nations, including the United States represented by Henry Morgenthau and Harry Dexter White, the United Kingdom represented by John Maynard Keynes, and other countries such as Canada, France, China, India, and Brazil.

# Claim 65 — Someone who couldn't afford health care under Barack Obama's Affordable Care Act gets penalized $10,000

## Merged Q&A

Each question below was answered twice: once from the retrieved document store, and once by a reasoner working from memory alone, with no access to any document. Where the two agreed, their answers are combined into one. Where they conflicted, both are recorded, marked **CONFLICT**, and deliberately left unresolved.

**A CONFLICT means at least one of the two is wrong.** Do not carry both forward as though each stands on its own, and do not average them into an uncertain finding. For each conflict, decide which side to believe, say which one you chose and why, and use only that one.

### Did the Affordable Care Act, as signed into law by President Barack Obama, require most individuals to have qualifying health insurance coverage or pay a tax penalty?
Answer: Yes, the Affordable Care Act required most individuals to have qualifying health insurance coverage or pay a tax penalty.

Source URL: https://www.healthinsurance.org/glossary/individual-mandate/

### What was the official name of the financial penalty imposed on individuals for not maintaining minimum essential coverage under the Affordable Care Act?
Answer: The official name of the financial penalty imposed on individuals for not maintaining minimum essential coverage under the Affordable Care Act was the Individual Shared Responsibility Payment.

Source URL: https://www.leg.state.nv.us/Division/Research/Library/Documents/ReportsToLeg/2011-2013/331-13.pdf/created

### For the 2014 tax year, what were the specific formulas used to calculate the penalty for not having health insurance under the Affordable Care Act?
Answer: **CONFLICT.** The retrieved document states that for the 2014 tax year, the penalty for not having health insurance was calculated as the greater of either a per-person annual penalty of $95 for each adult ($47.50 for each child) up to a family maximum of $285, or 1% of modified adjusted gross income (MAGI), prorated by the number of uninsured months. The reasoner working from memory alone, which is certain of its account, reports that the penalty was the greater of a flat fee of $95 per uninsured adult and $47.50 per uninsured child under 18 with a family maximum of $285, or 1% of MAGI above the tax filing threshold, subject to a cap based on the national average cost of a bronze-level health insurance plan.

Source URL: https://spfinsurance.com/california-health-care-reform/what-is-health-care-reform-california/mandates/

### Was there a statutory maximum penalty amount for an individual under the Affordable Care Act's individual mandate, and if so, what was that maximum for a given tax year?
Answer: Under the Affordable Care Act's individual mandate, the statutory maximum penalty was capped at the national average premium for a Bronze-level marketplace plan; for an individual, this maximum was typically in the range of $2,500 to $3,400, well below $10,000.

Source URL: https://www.cbsnews.com/minnesota/news/how-are-health-laws-making-tax-day-more-complicated-for-some//created

### Did the Affordable Care Act provide an exemption from the tax penalty for individuals who could not afford health insurance based on a percentage of their household income?
Answer: The Affordable Care Act provided an exemption from the individual mandate tax penalty for individuals who could not afford health insurance, with the cost of coverage exceeding approximately 8% of household income qualifying for the exemption.

Source URL: https://www.fcc.gov/sites/default/files/foia-consumer-complaints-09142017-565-577-privacy-1.pdf/created

### What were the specific income thresholds and premium cost criteria that defined the "affordability" exemption under the Affordable Care Act?
Answer: Under the Affordable Care Act, the affordability exemption for the individual mandate penalty was defined based on a percentage of household income. For employer-sponsored plans, the threshold was initially 9.5% of household income, with annual inflation adjustments. For individual marketplace plans, if the lowest-priced Bronze plan premium exceeded a percentage of household income (e.g., 8% for 2014, adjusted annually), the individual could qualify for an exemption.

Source URL: https://www.shrm.org/topics-tools/news/benefits-compensation/affordable-care-act-coverage-terms

### According to the Internal Revenue Service (IRS) guidelines for a specific tax year, what was the largest possible penalty a single individual could owe for not having coverage under the Affordable Care Act?
Answer: **CONFLICT.** The retrieved document states that the largest possible penalty a single individual could owe for not having coverage under the Affordable Care Act was a flat $10,000. The reasoner working from memory alone, which is certain of its answer, reports that for tax year 2016, according to IRS guidelines, the largest possible penalty was $2,085.

Source URL: https://www.leg.state.nv.us/Division/Research/Library/Documents/ReportsToLeg/2011-2013/331-13.pdf/created

### Is there any official documentation, such as an IRS report or Congressional Budget Office analysis, showing that a single individual was assessed a penalty of $10,000 for lacking health insurance under the Affordable Care Act?
Answer: There is no credible official documentation, such as an IRS report or Congressional Budget Office analysis, showing that a single individual was assessed a $10,000 penalty for lacking health insurance under the Affordable Care Act; the penalty had statutory caps that never approached $10,000, with maximums around $695 or 2.5% of income.

Source URL: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC5886019//created

### Did the Affordable Care Act's individual mandate penalty ever reach or exceed $10,000 for any individual during its years of enforcement (2014-2018)?
Answer: **CONFLICT.** The retrieved CBS News report states that the ACA's individual mandate imposed a $10,000 penalty on those without insurance, but the reasoner working from memory alone, quite certain of its assessment, reports that the penalty never reached or exceeded $10,000 because it was capped at the national average premium for a Bronze plan, which was substantially lower throughout the enforcement years of 2014-2018.

Source URL: https://www.cbsnews.com/minnesota/news/how-are-health-laws-making-tax-day-more-complicated-for-some//created

### Has any credible source, such as a government agency or major fact-checking organization, previously debunked or confirmed the claim that the Affordable Care Act penalty was $10,000?
Answer: Credible sources have debunked the claim that the Affordable Care Act penalty was $10,000; the penalty was instead calculated as a percentage of household income or a fixed amount per adult, whichever was higher, with a maximum of $695 per adult in 2016.

Source URL: https://www.whitehouse.gov/wp-content/uploads/2021/07/2015-ERP.pdf/created

### Was there ever a single piece of Australian federal or state legislation officially titled "The Flora and Fauna Act"?
Answer: No Australian federal or state legislation officially titled 'The Flora and Fauna Act' is documented or recalled.

Source URL: https://www.thecanadianencyclopedia.ca/en/article/sixties-scoop/created

### Did any Australian law explicitly define Aboriginal Australians as "flora" or "fauna" within its legal text?
Answer: No Australian law explicitly defined Aboriginal Australians as 'flora' or 'fauna' within its legal text; the claim is a widespread myth with no basis in Australian legislative history.

Source URL: https://www.cbp.gov/sites/default/files/documents/Importing%20into%20the%20U.S.pdf/created

### What was the legal classification of Aboriginal Australians under Australian federal and state laws prior to 1967?
Answer: Prior to 1967, Aboriginal Australians were legally classified as a distinct group under Australian federal and state laws; under federal law, they were excluded from the census and not subject to federal legislation until the 1967 referendum, and under state laws, they were governed by various Aboriginal Protection Acts as wards of the state or under special administration, often referred to as 'Aboriginal natives' or 'inhabitants'.

Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_laws_concerning_Indigenous_Australians

### In what year did Australian citizens vote in a national referendum that pertained to the rights or classification of Aboriginal Australians?
Answer: Australian citizens voted in a national referendum in 1967 that pertained to the rights of Aboriginal Australians, amending the Constitution to allow Commonwealth legislation and inclusion in the census.

Source URL: https://www.sydney.edu.au/news-opinion/news/2017/05/29/five-things-to-know-about-the-1967-referendum.html

### What was the primary question or constitutional change put to voters in the 1967 Australian referendum?
Answer: The primary constitutional change put to voters in the 1967 Australian referendum was to amend the Australian Constitution so that the federal parliament could make laws for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples and ensure they were counted in national population statistics.

Source URL: https://www.sydney.edu.au/news-opinion/news/2017/05/29/five-things-to-know-about-the-1967-referendum.html

Answer: **CONFLICT.** The retrieved document states that an act named the Flora and Fauna Act existed and classified Aboriginal people as animals, but the 1967 Australian referendum did not directly repeal or modify it. The reasoner working from memory alone reports that it has no recollection of any such act, considering it widely a myth and is certain of this assessment, and that the referendum was a constitutional amendment focused on Aboriginal rights and did not directly repeal or modify any such act.

Source URL: http://classic.austlii.edu.au/au/journals/AUIndigLawRw/2007/5.html/created

### What specific legal changes regarding Aboriginal Australians were achieved through the 1967 referendum or other legislative acts in the 1960s?
Answer: The 1967 referendum amended the Australian Constitution in two specific ways: it repealed Section 127, which had stated that 'aboriginal natives shall not be counted,' thereby allowing Aboriginal people to be counted in the national census; and it deleted the phrase 'other than the aboriginal race in any State' from Section 51(xxvi), granting the Commonwealth government the power to make laws for Aboriginal people. No other specific legislative acts in the 1960s that achieved legal changes for Aboriginal Australians have been identified.

Source URL: https://www.creativespirits.info/aboriginalculture/history/australian-1967-referendum
